# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: FreddyRodgers_emojichef

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system encodes text and binary files into emoji strings and decodes them back to their original data. It treats emoji characters as symbols in a configurable base alphabet, allowing ordinary bytes to be represented as compact emoji sequences. The system also supports optional compression, optional integrity hashing, file MIME preservation, and simple file-oriented encode/decode workflows.

## 2. Functional Requirement

Recipe selection controls the emoji alphabet size. The supported recipe names are `quick`, `light`, `classic`, and `gourmet`, with increasing capacities of 64, 128, 256, and 1024 symbols. The classic recipe represents 8 bits per emoji. Unknown recipes should fall back to classic behavior.

Text encoding should use UTF-8 bytes, optionally compress the byte stream, split the resulting bits into recipe-sized chunks, and map each chunk to an emoji symbol. Decoding should reverse this process and reject null input or symbols that are not part of the active emoji alphabet. Empty text should round-trip as an empty emoji string.

Compression mode `zlib` should use standard zlib-compatible compression and decompression. Compression mode `none` should leave bytes unchanged. Verification mode `sha256` should produce a lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 digest with zero padding; verification mode `none` should produce an empty digest.

Binary data should be wrapped in a JSON-compatible structure before emoji encoding. The binary content is stored as Base64 text, and MIME type and original byte size must be preserved. The serialized field names are `content`, `mime_type`, and `size`. Decoding binary emoji data should restore the byte array and MIME type.

Encoding statistics should report `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `actual_ratio`, `theoretical_ratio`, and `bits_per_emoji`. File encoding results should include `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `mime_type`, and `actual_ratio`; file decoding results should include `decoded_size` and `mime_type`. File information results should include size, MIME type, whether the file can be processed, and a suggested recipe, or an error with `can_process` set to false.

Recipe suggestion is based on file size: small files below 1 KB use `quick`, files below 10 KB use `light`, files below 100 KB use `classic`, and larger files use `gourmet`.

File handling should read raw bytes, infer MIME type from the file name when possible, fall back to `application/octet-stream` when unknown, and write bytes while creating missing parent directories.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: FreddyRodgers_emojichef

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system encodes text and binary files into emoji strings and decodes them back to their original data. It treats emoji characters as symbols in a configurable base alphabet, allowing ordinary bytes to be represented as compact emoji sequences. The system also supports optional compression, optional integrity hashing, file MIME preservation, and simple file-oriented encode/decode workflows.

## 2. Functional Requirement

Recipe selection controls the emoji alphabet size. The supported recipe names are `quick`, `light`, `classic`, and `gourmet`, with increasing capacities of 64, 128, 256, and 1024 symbols. The classic recipe represents 8 bits per emoji. Unknown recipes should fall back to classic behavior.

Text encoding should use UTF-8 bytes, optionally compress the byte stream, split the resulting bits into recipe-sized chunks, and map each chunk to an emoji symbol. Decoding should reverse this process and reject null input or symbols that are not part of the active emoji alphabet. Empty text should round-trip as an empty emoji string.

Compression mode `zlib` should use standard zlib-compatible compression and decompression. Compression mode `none` should leave bytes unchanged. Verification mode `sha256` should produce a lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 digest with zero padding; verification mode `none` should produce an empty digest.

Binary data should be wrapped in a JSON-compatible structure before emoji encoding. The binary content is stored as Base64 text, and MIME type and original byte size must be preserved. The serialized field names are `content`, `mime_type`, and `size`. Decoding binary emoji data should restore the byte array and MIME type.

Encoding statistics should report `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `actual_ratio`, `theoretical_ratio`, and `bits_per_emoji`. File encoding results should include `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `mime_type`, and `actual_ratio`; file decoding results should include `decoded_size` and `mime_type`. File information results should include size, MIME type, whether the file can be processed, and a suggested recipe, or an error with `can_process` set to false.

Recipe suggestion is based on file size: small files below 1 KB use `quick`, files below 10 KB use `light`, files below 100 KB use `classic`, and larger files use `gourmet`.

File handling should read raw bytes, infer MIME type from the file name when possible, fall back to `application/octet-stream` when unknown, and write bytes while creating missing parent directories.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Operation Specifications
EmojiCodec::suggestRecipe
Summary: Selects a recipe name based on file size.
Algorithm:
  1. If size < 1024, return "quick".
  2. If size < 10240, return "light".
  3. If size < 102400, return "classic".
  4. Otherwise, return "gourmet".
Related Features: none
Related Classes: None
Input: size : int - file size in bytes; must be non-negative.
Output: String - one of "quick", "light", "classic", or "gourmet".
Pre-condition: size >= 0.
Post-condition: The returned recipe matches the file-size thresholds.

EmojiCodec::decodeBinary
Summary: Decodes an emoji string representing a binary-data JSON wrapper and restores the original raw bytes and MIME type.
Algorithm:
  1. If encoded is null, reject.
  2. Call decode(encoded) to obtain the JSON text string.
  3. Parse the JSON text into an EMap containing content, mime_type, and size.
  4. Call restoreBinaryDataFromMap(map) to get the original byte array.
  5. Return a List<EJavaObject> containing the byte array first and the MIME type string second.
Related Features: decode, restoreBinaryDataFromMap
Related Classes: EMap, EByteArray, EJavaObject
Input: encoded : String - an emoji string produced by encodeBinary; must be non-null.
Output: List<EJavaObject> - a two-element list: raw byte array and MIME type string.
Pre-condition: encoded is a valid emoji string whose decoded text is a valid JSON binary wrapper.
Post-condition: The returned bytes and MIME type match the original binary data.

EmojiCodec::getFileInfo
Summary: Returns file information needed to decide whether a file can be processed.
Algorithm:
  1. Try to read the file with fileHandler.readFile(filePath).
  2. If reading fails, return an EMap with can_process=false and an error message.
  3. Otherwise, use the read FileData to obtain size and MIME type.
  4. Call suggestRecipe(size) to choose a recipe.
  5. Return an EMap containing size, mime_type, can_process=true, and suggested_recipe.
Related Features: fileHandler, suggestRecipe
Related Classes: FileHandler, FileData, EMap
Input: filePath : String - path to the file being inspected.
Output: EMap - with size, mime_type, can_process, suggested_recipe; on failure, can_process=false and error.
Pre-condition: filePath is non-null.
Post-condition: If the file is readable, a suggestion is returned; otherwise the error result clearly marks can_process=false.

EmojiCodec::calculateHash
Summary: Produces an integrity digest for a byte array according to the configured verification mode.
Algorithm:
  1. If verification equals VerificationMethod.none, return an empty string.
  2. Compute the SHA-256 digest over data.
  3. Convert the digest bytes to lowercase hexadecimal, left-padding with zeroes so the result has 64 characters.
Related Features: verification, verificationMethod
Related Classes: VerificationMethod, EByteArray
Input: data : EByteArray - the bytes to hash; must be non-null.
Output: String - lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 digest of length 64, or an empty string when verification mode is none.
Pre-condition: verification is either "none" or "sha256"; data is non-null.
Post-condition: For sha256, a 64-character lowercase hex string is returned; for none, an empty string is returned.

EmojiCodec::processData
Summary: Applies the configured compression mode to a byte array.
Algorithm:
  1. If compression equals CompressionMethod.none, return data as a copy.
  2. If compression equals CompressionMethod.zlib, compress data using standard zlib compression and return the compressed bytes.
Related Features: compression, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod, EByteArray
Input: data : EByteArray - raw bytes to process.
Output: EByteArray - processed bytes; unchanged for none, zlib-compressed for zlib.
Pre-condition: data is non-null; compressionMethod is a supported mode.
Post-condition: The returned bytes are the reversible processed form expected by unprocessData.

EmojiCodec::restoreBinaryDataFromMap
Summary: Restores original binary bytes from an EMap representing the JSON binary wrapper.
Algorithm:
  1. Read content from the map; it must be a Base64 string.
  2. Read size from the map; it must be the expected byte count.
  3. Base64-decode content.
  4. Verify the decoded length equals size.
  5. Return the decoded byte array.
Related Features: none
Related Classes: EMap, EByteArray
Input: data : EMap - map containing content, mime_type, and size fields.
Output: EByteArray - original binary bytes.
Pre-condition: data is non-null; required fields exist and are well-formed.
Post-condition: The returned byte array matches the size field.

EmojiCodec::encodeBinary
Summary: Wraps binary data in a JSON-compatible structure and encodes it as an emoji string.
Algorithm:
  1. Call prepareBinaryData(mimeType, data) to create a PreparedData object.
  2. Call toMap() on the PreparedData to obtain an EMap with content, mime_type, and size.
  3. Serialize the map as a JSON object string, e.g., {"content":"...","mime_type":"...","size":...}.
  4. Return encode(jsonString).
Related Features: prepareBinaryData, encode
Related Classes: PreparedData, EMap, EByteArray
Input: mimeType : String - MIME type of the binary data; data : EByteArray - raw binary bytes.
Output: String - an emoji string that represents the JSON-wrapped binary data.
Pre-condition: data is non-null; mimeType is non-null or defaulted to application/octet-stream.
Post-condition: decodeBinary of the returned string restores the original bytes and MIME type.

EmojiCodec::encode
Summary: Encodes UTF-8 text into an emoji string using the active recipe alphabet.
Algorithm:
  1. If data is null, reject.
  2. Convert data to raw UTF-8 bytes.
  3. If raw bytes are empty, return an empty emoji string.
  4. Call processData(raw) to optionally compress the byte stream.
  5. Prepend a 4-byte big-endian length prefix equal to the processed byte length.
  6. Convert the resulting payload to a bit stream, MSB first.
  7. Split the bit stream into bitsPerChunk-sized chunks, padding the final chunk with zero bits if needed.
  8. Map each chunk value through emojiMap and append the resulting emoji character.
Related Features: processData, emojiMap, bitsPerChunk, mask, baseSize, compression
Related Classes: EMap, EByteArray, CompressionMethod
Input: data : String - UTF-8 text to encode; must be non-null; empty allowed.
Output: String - emoji string using only active recipe symbols; empty for empty input.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec is initialized; recipeType, compression mode, and maps are configured.
Post-condition: decode(encode(data)) equals data; empty text remains an empty emoji string.

EmojiCodec::decode
Summary: Reverses an emoji-encoded text string back into the original UTF-8 text.
Algorithm:
  1. If emojiData is null, reject.
  2. If emojiData is empty, return an empty string.
  3. For each character in emojiData, look it up in reverseMap; reject if the character is absent.
  4. Convert each reverse-mapped chunk index to bitsPerChunk bits, MSB first, and concatenate them.
  5. Convert the bit stream to bytes, then parse the first 4 bytes as a big-endian length prefix.
  6. Keep exactly that many processed bytes after the prefix and discard padding bytes.
  7. Call unprocessData on those processed bytes and return the resulting bytes as a UTF-8 string.
Related Features: reverseMap, bitsPerChunk, mask, emojiMap, unprocessData, compression
Related Classes: EMap, EByteArray, CompressionMethod
Input: emojiData : String - an emoji string produced by encode, using only symbols of the active recipe; may be empty.
Output: String - the decoded UTF-8 text; empty if emojiData is empty.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec is initialized; emojiData is non-null; every character is in reverseMap.
Post-condition: If emojiData was produced by encode, the original text is returned; invalid symbols are rejected.

EmojiCodec::prepareBinaryData
Summary: Prepares binary data for JSON wrapping before emoji encoding.
Algorithm:
  1. content = Base64.encode(data).
  2. size = data.length.
  3. If mimeType is null, set it to "application/octet-stream".
  4. Return a new PreparedData(content, mimeType, size).
Related Features: none
Related Classes: PreparedData, EByteArray
Input: mimeType : String - MIME type of the data; data : EByteArray - raw binary content.
Output: PreparedData - contains Base64 content, MIME type, and original byte size.
Pre-condition: data is non-null; mimeType may be null and is then defaulted.
Post-condition: The PreparedData fields preserve the binary content, MIME type, and original size.

EmojiCodec::unprocessData
Summary: Reverses the compression applied by processData.
Algorithm:
  1. If compression equals CompressionMethod.none, return data as a copy.
  2. If compression equals CompressionMethod.zlib, decompress data using standard zlib decompression and return the original bytes.
Related Features: compression, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod, EByteArray
Input: data : EByteArray - processed bytes to reverse.
Output: EByteArray - original bytes restored by decompression.
Pre-condition: data is non-null; for zlib mode, data must be a valid zlib stream.
Post-condition: The returned bytes are the original input to processData.

EmojiCodec::getStats
Summary: Computes encoding statistics for a text encode operation.
Algorithm:
  1. originalBytes = original.getBytes(UTF_8).length.
  2. encodedLength = encoded.length().
  3. bitsPerEmoji = bitsPerChunk.
  4. theoreticalRatio = bitsPerEmoji / 8.0.
  5. actualRatio = originalBytes / encodedLength if encodedLength > 0, else 0.0.
  6. Return an EMap with original_bytes, encoded_length, actual_ratio, theoretical_ratio, and bits_per_emoji.
Related Features: bitsPerChunk
Related Classes: EMap
Input: original : String - the original text before encoding; encoded : String - the produced emoji string.
Output: EMap - statistics about the encoding.
Pre-condition: original is non-null; encoded is non-null; encoded was produced from original.
Post-condition: The map contains all five statistics with the given numeric meanings.

EmojiCodec::restoreBinaryData
Summary: Restores original binary bytes from a PreparedData object.
Algorithm:
  1. Base64-decode preparedData.content.
  2. If the decoded length does not equal preparedData.size, reject.
  3. Return the decoded byte array.
Related Features: none
Related Classes: PreparedData, EByteArray
Input: preparedData : PreparedData - prepared binary wrapper.
Output: EByteArray - original binary bytes.
Pre-condition: preparedData is non-null; content is valid Base64; size matches the content length.
Post-condition: The returned byte array is the original binary data.

EmojiCodec::processFile
Summary: Performs a file-oriented encode or decode workflow and returns result statistics.
Algorithm:
  1. If operation is "encode":
       a. Read the input file with fileHandler.readFile(inputPath).
       b. Call encodeBinary(fd.mimeType, fd.content).
       c. Write the emoji string bytes to outputPath using fileHandler.writeFile.
       d. Return an EMap with original_bytes, encoded_length, mime_type, and actual_ratio.
  2. If operation is "decode":
       a. Read the emoji text file with fileHandler.readFile(inputPath).
       b. Convert the file bytes to a UTF-8 string.
       c. Call decodeBinary(emojiText) to obtain bytes and MIME type.
       d. Write the decoded raw bytes to outputPath using fileHandler.writeFile.
       e. Return an EMap with decoded_size and mime_type.
  3. For any other operation value, reject.
Related Features: fileHandler, encodeBinary, decodeBinary
Related Classes: FileHandler, FileData, EMap, EByteArray, EJavaObject
Input: inputPath : String - source file path; outputPath : String - destination file path; operation : String - "encode" or "decode".
Output: EMap - file encoding or decoding result.
Pre-condition: inputPath is readable; outputPath may be missing parent directories; operation is valid.
Post-condition: The output file is written, missing parents are created, and the returned map contains the required fields.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: FreddyRodgers_emojichef

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system encodes text and binary files into emoji strings and decodes them back to their original data. It treats emoji characters as symbols in a configurable base alphabet, allowing ordinary bytes to be represented as compact emoji sequences. The system also supports optional compression, optional integrity hashing, file MIME preservation, and simple file-oriented encode/decode workflows.

## 2. Functional Requirement

Recipe selection controls the emoji alphabet size. The supported recipe names are `quick`, `light`, `classic`, and `gourmet`, with increasing capacities of 64, 128, 256, and 1024 symbols. The classic recipe represents 8 bits per emoji. Unknown recipes should fall back to classic behavior.

Text encoding should use UTF-8 bytes, optionally compress the byte stream, split the resulting bits into recipe-sized chunks, and map each chunk to an emoji symbol. Decoding should reverse this process and reject null input or symbols that are not part of the active emoji alphabet. Empty text should round-trip as an empty emoji string.

Compression mode `zlib` should use standard zlib-compatible compression and decompression. Compression mode `none` should leave bytes unchanged. Verification mode `sha256` should produce a lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 digest with zero padding; verification mode `none` should produce an empty digest.

Binary data should be wrapped in a JSON-compatible structure before emoji encoding. The binary content is stored as Base64 text, and MIME type and original byte size must be preserved. The serialized field names are `content`, `mime_type`, and `size`. Decoding binary emoji data should restore the byte array and MIME type.

Encoding statistics should report `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `actual_ratio`, `theoretical_ratio`, and `bits_per_emoji`. File encoding results should include `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `mime_type`, and `actual_ratio`; file decoding results should include `decoded_size` and `mime_type`. File information results should include size, MIME type, whether the file can be processed, and a suggested recipe, or an error with `can_process` set to false.

Recipe suggestion is based on file size: small files below 1 KB use `quick`, files below 10 KB use `light`, files below 100 KB use `classic`, and larger files use `gourmet`.

File handling should read raw bytes, infer MIME type from the file name when possible, fall back to `application/octet-stream` when unknown, and write bytes while creating missing parent directories.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Operation Specifications
EmojiCodec::suggestRecipe
Summary: Selects a recipe name based on file size.
Algorithm:
  1. If size < 1024, return "quick".
  2. If size < 10240, return "light".
  3. If size < 102400, return "classic".
  4. Otherwise, return "gourmet".
Related Features: none
Related Classes: None
Input: size : int - file size in bytes; must be non-negative.
Output: String - one of "quick", "light", "classic", or "gourmet".
Pre-condition: size >= 0.
Post-condition: The returned recipe matches the file-size thresholds.

EmojiCodec::decodeBinary
Summary: Decodes an emoji string representing a binary-data JSON wrapper and restores the original raw bytes and MIME type.
Algorithm:
  1. If encoded is null, reject.
  2. Call decode(encoded) to obtain the JSON text string.
  3. Parse the JSON text into an EMap containing content, mime_type, and size.
  4. Call restoreBinaryDataFromMap(map) to get the original byte array.
  5. Return a List<EJavaObject> containing the byte array first and the MIME type string second.
Related Features: decode, restoreBinaryDataFromMap
Related Classes: EMap, EByteArray, EJavaObject
Input: encoded : String - an emoji string produced by encodeBinary; must be non-null.
Output: List<EJavaObject> - a two-element list: raw byte array and MIME type string.
Pre-condition: encoded is a valid emoji string whose decoded text is a valid JSON binary wrapper.
Post-condition: The returned bytes and MIME type match the original binary data.

EmojiCodec::getFileInfo
Summary: Returns file information needed to decide whether a file can be processed.
Algorithm:
  1. Try to read the file with fileHandler.readFile(filePath).
  2. If reading fails, return an EMap with can_process=false and an error message.
  3. Otherwise, use the read FileData to obtain size and MIME type.
  4. Call suggestRecipe(size) to choose a recipe.
  5. Return an EMap containing size, mime_type, can_process=true, and suggested_recipe.
Related Features: fileHandler, suggestRecipe
Related Classes: FileHandler, FileData, EMap
Input: filePath : String - path to the file being inspected.
Output: EMap - with size, mime_type, can_process, suggested_recipe; on failure, can_process=false and error.
Pre-condition: filePath is non-null.
Post-condition: If the file is readable, a suggestion is returned; otherwise the error result clearly marks can_process=false.

EmojiCodec::calculateHash
Summary: Produces an integrity digest for a byte array according to the configured verification mode.
Algorithm:
  1. If verification equals VerificationMethod.none, return an empty string.
  2. Compute the SHA-256 digest over data.
  3. Convert the digest bytes to lowercase hexadecimal, left-padding with zeroes so the result has 64 characters.
Related Features: verification, verificationMethod
Related Classes: VerificationMethod, EByteArray
Input: data : EByteArray - the bytes to hash; must be non-null.
Output: String - lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 digest of length 64, or an empty string when verification mode is none.
Pre-condition: verification is either "none" or "sha256"; data is non-null.
Post-condition: For sha256, a 64-character lowercase hex string is returned; for none, an empty string is returned.

EmojiCodec::processData
Summary: Applies the configured compression mode to a byte array.
Algorithm:
  1. If compression equals CompressionMethod.none, return data as a copy.
  2. If compression equals CompressionMethod.zlib, compress data using standard zlib compression and return the compressed bytes.
Related Features: compression, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod, EByteArray
Input: data : EByteArray - raw bytes to process.
Output: EByteArray - processed bytes; unchanged for none, zlib-compressed for zlib.
Pre-condition: data is non-null; compressionMethod is a supported mode.
Post-condition: The returned bytes are the reversible processed form expected by unprocessData.

EmojiCodec::restoreBinaryDataFromMap

  4. Verify the decoded length equals size.

  4. Convert each reverse-mapped chunk index to bitsPerChunk bits, MSB first, and concatenate them.
  5. Convert the concatenated bit stream to bytes, padding the final partial byte with zero bits if needed.
  6. Parse the first 4 bytes as a big-endian length prefix.
  7. Keep exactly that many processed bytes after the prefix and discard padding bytes.
  8. Call unprocessData on those processed bytes and return the resulting bytes as a UTF-8 string.
Related Features: reverseMap, bitsPerChunk, unprocessData
Related Classes: EMap, EByteArray, CompressionMethod
Input: emojiData : String - an emoji string to decode; null and invalid symbols are rejected; empty returns empty.
Output: String - the decoded UTF-8 text; empty if emojiData is empty.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec is initialized.

  5. Read the mime_type value from the map as a String.
  6. Return a List<EJavaObject> containing the byte array first and the MIME type string second.
Related Features: decode, restoreBinaryDataFromMap
Related Classes: EMap, EByteArray, EJavaObject
Input: encoded : String - an emoji string produced by encodeBinary; null is rejected.
Output: List<EJavaObject> - a two-element list: raw byte array and MIME type string.

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: FreddyRodgers_emojichef

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system encodes text and binary files into emoji strings and decodes them back to their original data. It treats emoji characters as symbols in a configurable base alphabet, allowing ordinary bytes to be represented as compact emoji sequences. The system also supports optional compression, optional integrity hashing, file MIME preservation, and simple file-oriented encode/decode workflows.

## 2. Functional Requirement

Recipe selection controls the emoji alphabet size. The supported recipe names are `quick`, `light`, `classic`, and `gourmet`, with increasing capacities of 64, 128, 256, and 1024 symbols. The classic recipe represents 8 bits per emoji. Unknown recipes should fall back to classic behavior.

Text encoding should use UTF-8 bytes, optionally compress the byte stream, split the resulting bits into recipe-sized chunks, and map each chunk to an emoji symbol. Decoding should reverse this process and reject null input or symbols that are not part of the active emoji alphabet. Empty text should round-trip as an empty emoji string.

Compression mode `zlib` should use standard zlib-compatible compression and decompression. Compression mode `none` should leave bytes unchanged. Verification mode `sha256` should produce a lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 digest with zero padding; verification mode `none` should produce an empty digest.

Binary data should be wrapped in a JSON-compatible structure before emoji encoding. The binary content is stored as Base64 text, and MIME type and original byte size must be preserved. The serialized field names are `content`, `mime_type`, and `size`. Decoding binary emoji data should restore the byte array and MIME type.

Encoding statistics should report `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `actual_ratio`, `theoretical_ratio`, and `bits_per_emoji`. File encoding results should include `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `mime_type`, and `actual_ratio`; file decoding results should include `decoded_size` and `mime_type`. File information results should include size, MIME type, whether the file can be processed, and a suggested recipe, or an error with `can_process` set to false.

Recipe suggestion is based on file size: small files below 1 KB use `quick`, files below 10 KB use `light`, files below 100 KB use `classic`, and larger files use `gourmet`.

File handling should read raw bytes, infer MIME type from the file name when possible, fall back to `application/octet-stream` when unknown, and write bytes while creating missing parent directories.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Operation Specifications
EmojiCodec::suggestRecipe
Summary: Selects a recipe name based on file size.
Algorithm:
  1. If size < 1024, return "quick".
  2. If size < 10240, return "light".
  3. If size < 102400, return "classic".
  4. Otherwise, return "gourmet".
Related Features: none
Related Classes: None
Input: size : int - file size in bytes; must be non-negative.
Output: String - one of "quick", "light", "classic", or "gourmet".
Pre-condition: size >= 0.
Post-condition: The returned recipe matches the file-size thresholds.

EmojiCodec::decodeBinary
Summary: Decodes an emoji string representing a binary-data JSON wrapper and restores the original raw bytes and MIME type.
Algorithm:
  1. If encoded is null, reject.
  2. Call decode(encoded) to obtain the JSON text string.
  3. Parse the JSON text into an EMap containing content, mime_type, and size.
  4. Call restoreBinaryDataFromMap(map) to obtain the original byte array.
  5. Read the mime_type value from the map as a String.
  6. Return a List<EJavaObject> containing the byte array first and the MIME type string second.
Related Features: decode, restoreBinaryDataFromMap
Related Classes: EMap, EByteArray, EJavaObject
Input: encoded : String - an emoji string produced by encodeBinary; null is rejected.
Output: List<EJavaObject> - a two-element list: raw byte array and MIME type string.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec is initialized; encoded is a valid emoji string whose decoded text is a valid JSON binary wrapper.
Post-condition: The returned bytes and MIME type match the original binary data.

EmojiCodec::getFileInfo
Summary: Returns file information needed to decide whether a file can be processed.
Algorithm:
  1. Try to read the file with fileHandler.readFile(filePath).
  2. If reading fails, return an EMap with can_process=false and an error message.
  3. Otherwise, use the read FileData to obtain size and MIME type.
  4. Call suggestRecipe(size) to choose a recipe.
  5. Return an EMap containing size, mime_type, can_process=true, and suggested_recipe.
Related Features: fileHandler, suggestRecipe
Related Classes: FileHandler, FileData, EMap
Input: filePath : String - path to the file being inspected.
Output: EMap - with size, mime_type, can_process, suggested_recipe; on failure, can_process=false and error.
Pre-condition: filePath is non-null.
Post-condition: If the file is readable, a suggestion is returned; otherwise the error result clearly marks can_process=false.

EmojiCodec::calculateHash
Summary: Produces an integrity digest for a byte array according to the configured verification mode.
Algorithm:
  1. If verification equals VerificationMethod.none, return an empty string.
  2. Compute the SHA-256 digest over data.
  3. Convert the digest bytes to lowercase hexadecimal, left-padding with zeroes so the result has 64 characters.
Related Features: verification, verificationMethod
Related Classes: VerificationMethod, EByteArray
Input: data : EByteArray - the bytes to hash; must be non-null.
Output: String - lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 digest of length 64, or an empty string when verification mode is none.
Pre-condition: verification is either "none" or "sha256"; data is non-null.
Post-condition: For sha256, a 64-character lowercase hex string is returned; for none, an empty string is returned.

EmojiCodec::processData
Summary: Applies the configured compression mode to a byte array.
Algorithm:
  1. If compression equals CompressionMethod.none, return data as a copy.
  2. If compression equals CompressionMethod.zlib, compress data using standard zlib compression and return the compressed bytes.
Related Features: compression, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod, EByteArray
Input: data : EByteArray - raw bytes to process.
Output: EByteArray - processed bytes; unchanged for none, zlib-compressed for zlib.
Pre-condition: data is non-null; compressionMethod is a supported mode.
Post-condition: The returned bytes are the reversible processed form expected by unprocessData.

EmojiCodec::restoreBinaryDataFromMap
Summary: Restores original binary bytes from an EMap representing the JSON binary wrapper.
Algorithm:
  1. Read content from the map; it must be a Base64 string.
  2. Read size from the map; it must be the expected byte count.
  3. Base64-decode content.
  4. Verify the decoded length equals size.
  5. Return the decoded byte array.
Related Features: none
Related Classes: EMap, EByteArray
Input: data : EMap - map containing content, mime_type, and size fields.
Output: EByteArray - original binary bytes.
Pre-condition: data is non-null; required fields exist and are well-formed.
Post-condition: The returned byte array matches the size field.

EmojiCodec::encodeBinary
Summary: Wraps binary data in a JSON-compatible structure and encodes it as an emoji string.
Algorithm:
  1. Call prepareBinaryData(mimeType, data) to create a PreparedData object.
  2. Call toMap() on the PreparedData to obtain an EMap with content, mime_type, and size.
  3. Serialize the map as a JSON object string, e.g., {"content":"...","mime_type":"...","size":...}.
  4. Return encode(jsonString).
Related Features: prepareBinaryData, encode
Related Classes: PreparedData, EMap, EByteArray
Input: mimeType : String - MIME type of the binary data; data : EByteArray - raw binary bytes.
Output: String - an emoji string that represents the JSON-wrapped binary data.
Pre-condition: data is non-null; mimeType is non-null or defaulted to application/octet-stream.
Post-condition: decodeBinary of the returned string restores the original bytes and MIME type.

EmojiCodec::encode
Summary: Encodes UTF-8 text into an emoji string using the active recipe alphabet.
Algorithm:
  1. If data is null, reject.
  2. Convert data to raw UTF-8 bytes.
  3. If raw bytes are empty, return an empty emoji string.
  4. Call processData(raw) to optionally compress the byte stream.
  5. Prepend a 4-byte big-endian length prefix equal to the processed byte length.
  6. Convert the resulting payload to a bit stream, MSB first.
  7. Split the bit stream into bitsPerChunk-sized chunks, padding the final chunk with zero bits if needed.
  8. Map each chunk value through emojiMap and append the resulting emoji character.
Related Features: processData, emojiMap, bitsPerChunk, mask, baseSize, compression
Related Classes: EMap, EByteArray, CompressionMethod
Input: data : String - UTF-8 text to encode; must be non-null; empty allowed.
Output: String - emoji string using only active recipe symbols; empty for empty input.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec is initialized; recipeType, compression mode, and maps are configured.
Post-condition: decode(encode(data)) equals data; empty text remains an empty emoji string.

EmojiCodec::decode
Summary: Reverses an emoji-encoded text string back into the original UTF-8 text.
Algorithm:
  1. If emojiData is null, reject.
  2. If emojiData is empty, return an empty string.
  3. For each Unicode code point (emoji symbol) in emojiData, look it up in reverseMap; reject if the symbol is absent.
  4. Convert each reverse-mapped chunk index to bitsPerChunk bits, MSB first, and concatenate them.
  5. Convert the concatenated bit stream to bytes, padding the final partial byte with zero bits if needed.
  6. Parse the first 4 bytes as a big-endian length prefix.
  7. Keep exactly that many processed bytes after the prefix and discard padding bytes.
  8. Call unprocessData on those processed bytes and return the resulting bytes as a UTF-8 string.
Related Features: reverseMap, bitsPerChunk, unprocessData
Related Classes: EMap, EByteArray, CompressionMethod
Input: emojiData : String - an emoji string to decode; null and invalid symbols are rejected; empty returns empty.
Output: String - the decoded UTF-8 text; empty if emojiData is empty.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec is initialized.
Post-condition: If emojiData is null or contains a symbol absent from the active reverseMap, the operation rejects; otherwise, if emojiData was produced by encode, the original text is returned.

EmojiCodec::prepareBinaryData
Summary: Prepares binary data for JSON wrapping before emoji encoding.
Algorithm:
  1. content = Base64.encode(data).
  2. size = data.length.
  3. If mimeType is null, set it to "application/octet-stream".
  4. Return a new PreparedData(content, mimeType, size).
Related Features: none
Related Classes: PreparedData, EByteArray
Input: mimeType : String - MIME type of the data; data : EByteArray - raw binary content.
Output: PreparedData - contains Base64 content, MIME type, and original byte size.
Pre-condition: data is non-null; mimeType may be null and is then defaulted.
Post-condition: The PreparedData fields preserve the binary content, MIME type, and original size.

EmojiCodec::unprocessData
Summary: Reverses the compression applied by processData.
Algorithm:
  1. If compression equals CompressionMethod.none, return data as a copy.
  2. If compression equals CompressionMethod.zlib, decompress data using standard zlib decompression and return the original bytes.
Related Features: compression, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod, EByteArray
Input: data : EByteArray - processed bytes to reverse.
Output: EByteArray - original bytes restored by decompression.
Pre-condition: data is non-null; for zlib mode, data must be a valid zlib stream.
Post-condition: The returned bytes are the original input to processData.

EmojiCodec::getStats
Summary: Computes encoding statistics for a text encode operation.
Algorithm:
  1. originalBytes = original.getBytes(UTF_8).length.
  2. encodedLength = number of emoji symbols in encoded, using Unicode code point count.
  3. bitsPerEmoji = bitsPerChunk.
  4. theoreticalRatio = bitsPerEmoji / 8.0.
  5. actualRatio = encodedLength > 0 ? (double) originalBytes / encodedLength : 0.0.
  6. Return an EMap with original_bytes, encoded_length, actual_ratio, theoretical_ratio, and bits_per_emoji.
Related Features: bitsPerChunk
Related Classes: EMap
Input: original : String - the original text before encoding; encoded : String - the produced emoji string.
Output: EMap - statistics about the encoding.
Pre-condition: original is non-null; encoded is non-null; encoded was produced from original.
Post-condition: The map contains all five statistics with the given numeric meanings.

EmojiCodec::restoreBinaryData
Summary: Restores original binary bytes from a PreparedData object.
Algorithm:
  1. Base64-decode preparedData.content.
  2. If the decoded length does not equal preparedData.size, reject.
  3. Return the decoded byte array.
Related Features: none
Related Classes: PreparedData, EByteArray
Input: preparedData : PreparedData - prepared binary wrapper.
Output: EByteArray - original binary bytes.
Pre-condition: preparedData is non-null; content is valid Base64; size matches the content length.
Post-condition: The returned byte array is the original binary data.

EmojiCodec::processFile
Summary: Performs a file-oriented encode or decode workflow and returns result statistics.
Algorithm:
  1. If operation is "encode":
     a. Read the input file with fileHandler.readFile(inputPath), obtaining FileData fd.
     b. Set originalBytes = fd.content.length.
     c. Call encodeBinary(fd.mimeType, fd.content) to obtain encodedEmoji.
     d. Set encodedLength = number of emoji symbols in encodedEmoji.
     e. Set mimeType = fd.mimeType.
     f. Set actualRatio = encodedLength > 0 ? (double) originalBytes / encodedLength : 0.0.
     g. Write encodedEmoji.getBytes(UTF_8) to outputPath using fileHandler.writeFile.
     h. Return an EMap with original_bytes=originalBytes, encoded_length=encodedLength, mime_type=mimeType, and actual_ratio=actualRatio.
  2. If operation is "decode":
     a. Read the emoji text file with fileHandler.readFile(inputPath), obtaining FileData fd.
     b. Convert fd.content to a UTF-8 string emojiText.
     c. Call decodeBinary(emojiText) to obtain a List<EJavaObject> result; let decodedBytes be the byte array and mimeType be the MIME type string.
     d. Set decodedSize = decodedBytes.length.
     e. Write decodedBytes to outputPath using fileHandler.writeFile.
     f. Return an EMap with decoded_size=decodedSize and mime_type=mimeType.
  3. For any other operation value, reject.
Related Features: fileHandler, encodeBinary, decodeBinary
Related Classes: FileHandler, FileData, EMap, EByteArray, EJavaObject
Input: inputPath : String - source file path; outputPath : String - destination file path; operation : String - "encode" or "decode".
Output: EMap - file encoding or decoding result.
Pre-condition: inputPath is readable; outputPath may be missing parent directories; operation is valid.
Post-condition: The output file is written, missing parents are created, and the returned map contains the required fields.

```plantuml
    prepareBinaryData(mimeType : String, data : EByteArray) : PreparedData
    processData(data : EByteArray) : EByteArray
    processFile(inputPath : String, outputPath : String, operation : String) : EMap
    restoreBinaryData(preparedData : PreparedData) : EByteArray
    restoreBinaryDataFromMap(data : EMap) : EByteArray
    suggestRecipe(size : int) : String
    unprocessData(data : EByteArray) : EByteArray
}
class FileData {
    content : EByteArray
    mimeType : String
    equals(o : EJavaObject) : boolean
    hashCode() : int
    toString() : String
}
class FileHandler {
    createFileData(mimeType : String, content : EByteArray) : FileData
    guessMimeType(path : Path) : String
    readFile(filePath : String) : FileData
    writeFile(filePath : String, content : EByteArray) : void
}
class PreparedData {
    content : String
    mimeType : String
    size : int
    toMap() : EMap
}
class VerificationMethod {
    none : String
    sha256 : String
}

EmojiCodec --> CompressionMethod : compressionMethod
EmojiCodec --> FileHandler : fileHandler
EmojiCodec --> VerificationMethod : verificationMethod
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
EmojiCodec::suggestRecipe
Summary: Selects a recipe name based on file size.
Algorithm:
  1. If size < 1024, return "quick".
  2. If size < 10240, return "light".
  3. If size < 102400, return "classic".
  4. Otherwise, return "gourmet".
Related Features: none
Related Classes: None
Input: size : int - file size in bytes; must be non-negative.
Output: String - one of "quick", "light", "classic", or "gourmet".
Pre-condition: size >= 0.
Post-condition: The returned recipe matches the file-size thresholds.

EmojiCodec::decodeBinary
Summary: Decodes an emoji string representing a binary-data JSON wrapper and restores the original raw bytes and MIME type.
Algorithm:
  1. If encoded is null, reject.
  2. Call decode(encoded) to obtain the JSON text string.
  3. Parse the JSON text into an EMap containing content, mime_type, and size.
  4. Call restoreBinaryDataFromMap(map) to obtain the original byte array.
  5. Read the mime_type value from the map as a String.
  6. Return a List<EJavaObject> containing the byte array first and the MIME type string second.
Related Features: decode, restoreBinaryDataFromMap
Related Classes: EMap, EByteArray, EJavaObject
Input: encoded : String - an emoji string produced by encodeBinary; null is rejected.
Output: List<EJavaObject> - a two-element list: raw byte array and MIME type string.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec is initialized; encoded is a valid emoji string whose decoded text is a valid JSON binary wrapper.
Post-condition: The returned bytes and MIME type match the original binary data.

EmojiCodec::getFileInfo
Summary: Returns file information needed to decide whether a file can be processed.
Algorithm:
  1. Try to read the file with fileHandler.readFile(filePath).
  2. If reading fails, return an EMap with can_process=false and an error message.
  3. Otherwise, use the read FileData to obtain size and MIME type.
  4. Call suggestRecipe(size) to choose a recipe.
  5. Return an EMap containing size, mime_type, can_process=true, and suggested_recipe.
Related Features: fileHandler, suggestRecipe
Related Classes: FileHandler, FileData, EMap
Input: filePath : String - path to the file being inspected.
Output: EMap - with size, mime_type, can_process, suggested_recipe; on failure, can_process=false and error.
Pre-condition: filePath is non-null.
Post-condition: If the file is readable, a suggestion is returned; otherwise the error result clearly marks can_process=false.

EmojiCodec::calculateHash
Summary: Produces an integrity digest for a byte array according to the configured verification mode.
Algorithm:
  1. If verification equals VerificationMethod.none, return an empty string.
  2. Compute the SHA-256 digest over data.
  3. Convert the digest bytes to lowercase hexadecimal, left-padding with zeroes so the result has 64 characters.
Related Features: verification, verificationMethod
Related Classes: VerificationMethod, EByteArray
Input: data : EByteArray - the bytes to hash; must be non-null.
Output: String - lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 digest of length 64, or an empty string when verification mode is none.
Pre-condition: verification is either "none" or "sha256"; data is non-null.
Post-condition: For sha256, a 64-character lowercase hex string is returned; for none, an empty string is returned.

EmojiCodec::processData
Summary: Applies the configured compression mode to a byte array.
Algorithm:
  1. If compression equals CompressionMethod.none, return data as a copy.
  2. If compression equals CompressionMethod.zlib, compress data using standard zlib compression and return the compressed bytes.
Related Features: compression, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod, EByteArray
Input: data : EByteArray - raw bytes to process.
Output: EByteArray - processed bytes; unchanged for none, zlib-compressed for zlib.
Pre-condition: data is non-null; compressionMethod is a supported mode.
Post-condition: The returned bytes are the reversible processed form expected by unprocessData.

EmojiCodec::restoreBinaryDataFromMap
Summary: Restores original binary bytes from an EMap representing the JSON binary wrapper.
Algorithm:
  1. Read content from the map; it must be a Base64 string.
  2. Read size from the map; it must be the expected byte count.
  3. Base64-decode content.
  4. Verify the decoded length equals size.
  5. Return the decoded byte array.
Related Features: none
Related Classes: EMap, EByteArray
Input: data : EMap - map containing content, mime_type, and size fields.
Output: EByteArray - original binary bytes.
Pre-condition: data is non-null; required fields exist and are well-formed.
Post-condition: The returned byte array matches the size field.

EmojiCodec::encodeBinary
Summary: Wraps binary data in a JSON-compatible structure and encodes it as an emoji string.
Algorithm:
  1. Call prepareBinaryData(mimeType, data) to create a PreparedData object.
  2. Call toMap() on the PreparedData to obtain an EMap with content, mime_type, and size.
  3. Serialize the map as a JSON object string, e.g., {"content":"...","mime_type":"...","size":...}.
  4. Return encode(jsonString).
Related Features: prepareBinaryData, encode
Related Classes: PreparedData, EMap, EByteArray
Input: mimeType : String - MIME type of the binary data; data : EByteArray - raw binary bytes.
Output: String - an emoji string that represents the JSON-wrapped binary data.
Pre-condition: data is non-null; mimeType is non-null or defaulted to application/octet-stream.
Post-condition: decodeBinary of the returned string restores the original bytes and MIME type.

EmojiCodec::encode
Summary: Encodes UTF-8 text into an emoji string using the active recipe alphabet.
Algorithm:
  1. If data is null, reject.
  2. Convert data to raw UTF-8 bytes.
  3. If raw bytes are empty, return an empty emoji string.
  4. Call processData(raw) to optionally compress the byte stream.
  5. Prepend a 4-byte big-endian length prefix equal to the processed byte length.
  6. Convert the resulting payload to a bit stream, MSB first.
  7. Split the bit stream into bitsPerChunk-sized chunks, padding the final chunk with zero bits if needed.
  8. Map each chunk value through emojiMap and append the resulting emoji character.
Related Features: processData, emojiMap, bitsPerChunk, mask, baseSize, compression
Related Classes: EMap, EByteArray, CompressionMethod
Input: data : String - UTF-8 text to encode; must be non-null; empty allowed.
Output: String - emoji string using only active recipe symbols; empty for empty input.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec is initialized; recipeType, compression mode, and maps are configured.
Post-condition: decode(encode(data)) equals data; empty text remains an empty emoji string.

EmojiCodec::decode
Summary: Reverses an emoji-encoded text string back into the original UTF-8 text.
Algorithm:
  1. If emojiData is null, reject.
  2. If emojiData is empty, return an empty string.
  3. For each Unicode code point (emoji symbol) in emojiData, look it up in reverseMap; reject if the symbol is absent.
  4. Convert each reverse-mapped chunk index to bitsPerChunk bits, MSB first, and concatenate them.
  5. Convert the concatenated bit stream to bytes, padding the final partial byte with zero bits if needed.
  6. Parse the first 4 bytes as a big-endian length prefix.
  7. Keep exactly that many processed bytes after the prefix and discard padding bytes.
  8. Call unprocessData on those processed bytes and return the resulting bytes as a UTF-8 string.
Related Features: reverseMap, bitsPerChunk, unprocessData
Related Classes: EMap, EByteArray, CompressionMethod
Input: emojiData : String - an emoji string to decode; null and invalid symbols are rejected; empty returns empty.
Output: String - the decoded UTF-8 text; empty if emojiData is empty.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec is initialized.
Post-condition: If emojiData is null or contains a symbol absent from the active reverseMap, the operation rejects; otherwise, if emojiData was produced by encode, the original text is returned.

EmojiCodec::prepareBinaryData
Summary: Prepares binary data for JSON wrapping before emoji encoding.
Algorithm:
  1. content = Base64.encode(data).
  2. size = data.length.
  3. If mimeType is null, set it to "application/octet-stream".
  4. Return a new PreparedData(content, mimeType, size).
Related Features: none
Related Classes: PreparedData, EByteArray
Input: mimeType : String - MIME type of the data; data : EByteArray - raw binary content.
Output: PreparedData - contains Base64 content, MIME type, and original byte size.
Pre-condition: data is non-null; mimeType may be null and is then defaulted.
Post-condition: The PreparedData fields preserve the binary content, MIME type, and original size.

EmojiCodec::unprocessData
Summary: Reverses the compression applied by processData.
Algorithm:
  1. If compression equals CompressionMethod.none, return data as a copy.
  2. If compression equals CompressionMethod.zlib, decompress data using standard zlib decompression and return the original bytes.
Related Features: compression, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod, EByteArray
Input: data : EByteArray - processed bytes to reverse.
Output: EByteArray - original bytes restored by decompression.
Pre-condition: data is non-null; for zlib mode, data must be a valid zlib stream.
Post-condition: The returned bytes are the original input to processData.

EmojiCodec::getStats
Summary: Computes encoding statistics for a text encode operation.
Algorithm:
  1. originalBytes = original.getBytes(UTF_8).length.
  2. encodedLength = number of emoji symbols in encoded, using Unicode code point count.
  3. bitsPerEmoji = bitsPerChunk.
  4. theoreticalRatio = bitsPerEmoji / 8.0.
  5. actualRatio = encodedLength > 0 ? (double) originalBytes / encodedLength : 0.0.
  6. Return an EMap with original_bytes, encoded_length, actual_ratio, theoretical_ratio, and bits_per_emoji.
Related Features: bitsPerChunk
Related Classes: EMap
Input: original : String - the original text before encoding; encoded : String - the produced emoji string.
Output: EMap - statistics about the encoding.
Pre-condition: original is non-null; encoded is non-null; encoded was produced from original.
Post-condition: The map contains all five statistics with the given numeric meanings.

EmojiCodec::restoreBinaryData
Summary: Restores original binary bytes from a PreparedData object.
Algorithm:
  1. Base64-decode preparedData.content.
  2. If the decoded length does not equal preparedData.size, reject.
  3. Return the decoded byte array.
Related Features: none
Related Classes: PreparedData, EByteArray
Input: preparedData : PreparedData - prepared binary wrapper.
Output: EByteArray - original binary bytes.
Pre-condition: preparedData is non-null; content is valid Base64; size matches the content length.
Post-condition: The returned byte array is the original binary data.

EmojiCodec::processFile
Summary: Performs a file-oriented encode or decode workflow and returns result statistics.
Algorithm:
  1. If operation is "encode":
     a. Read the input file with fileHandler.readFile(inputPath), obtaining FileData fd.
     b. Set originalBytes = fd.content.length.
     c. Call encodeBinary(fd.mimeType, fd.content) to obtain encodedEmoji.
     d. Set encodedLength = number of emoji symbols in encodedEmoji.
     e. Set mimeType = fd.mimeType.
     f. Set actualRatio = encodedLength > 0 ? (double) originalBytes / encodedLength : 0.0.
     g. Write encodedEmoji.getBytes(UTF_8) to outputPath using fileHandler.writeFile.
     h. Return an EMap with original_bytes=originalBytes, encoded_length=encodedLength, mime_type=mimeType, and actual_ratio=actualRatio.
  2. If operation is "decode":
     a. Read the emoji text file with fileHandler.readFile(inputPath), obtaining FileData fd.
     b. Convert fd.content to a UTF-8 string emojiText.
     c. Call decodeBinary(emojiText) to obtain a List<EJavaObject> result; let decodedBytes be the byte array and mimeType be the MIME type string.
     d. Set decodedSize = decodedBytes.length.
     e. Write decodedBytes to outputPath using fileHandler.writeFile.
     f. Return an EMap with decoded_size=decodedSize and mime_type=mimeType.
  3. For any other operation value, reject.
Related Features: fileHandler, encodeBinary, decodeBinary
Related Classes: FileHandler, FileData, EMap, EByteArray, EJavaObject
Input: inputPath : String - source file path; outputPath : String - destination file path; operation : String - "encode" or "decode".
Output: EMap - file encoding or decoding result.
Pre-condition: inputPath is readable; outputPath may be missing parent directories; operation is valid.
Post-condition: The output file is written, missing parents are created, and the returned map contains the required fields.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: FreddyRodgers_emojichef

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system encodes text and binary files into emoji strings and decodes them back to their original data. It treats emoji characters as symbols in a configurable base alphabet, allowing ordinary bytes to be represented as compact emoji sequences. The system also supports optional compression, optional integrity hashing, file MIME preservation, and simple file-oriented encode/decode workflows.

## 2. Functional Requirement

Recipe selection controls the emoji alphabet size. The supported recipe names are `quick`, `light`, `classic`, and `gourmet`, with increasing capacities of 64, 128, 256, and 1024 symbols. The classic recipe represents 8 bits per emoji. Unknown recipes should fall back to classic behavior.

Text encoding should use UTF-8 bytes, optionally compress the byte stream, split the resulting bits into recipe-sized chunks, and map each chunk to an emoji symbol. Decoding should reverse this process and reject null input or symbols that are not part of the active emoji alphabet. Empty text should round-trip as an empty emoji string.

Compression mode `zlib` should use standard zlib-compatible compression and decompression. Compression mode `none` should leave bytes unchanged. Verification mode `sha256` should produce a lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 digest with zero padding; verification mode `none` should produce an empty digest.

Binary data should be wrapped in a JSON-compatible structure before emoji encoding. The binary content is stored as Base64 text, and MIME type and original byte size must be preserved. The serialized field names are `content`, `mime_type`, and `size`. Decoding binary emoji data should restore the byte array and MIME type.

Encoding statistics should report `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `actual_ratio`, `theoretical_ratio`, and `bits_per_emoji`. File encoding results should include `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `mime_type`, and `actual_ratio`; file decoding results should include `decoded_size` and `mime_type`. File information results should include size, MIME type, whether the file can be processed, and a suggested recipe, or an error with `can_process` set to false.

Recipe suggestion is based on file size: small files below 1 KB use `quick`, files below 10 KB use `light`, files below 100 KB use `classic`, and larger files use `gourmet`.

File handling should read raw bytes, infer MIME type from the file name when possible, fall back to `application/octet-stream` when unknown, and write bytes while creating missing parent directories.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Operation Specifications
FileData::hashCode
Summary: Computes a hash code for this FileData.
Algorithm:
  1. Compute a hash of the content byte array.
  2. Combine it with the hash of mimeType using e.g. 31 * contentHash + mimeTypeHash.
  3. Return the resulting int.
Related Features: content, mimeType
Related Classes: FileData, EByteArray
Input: None.
Output: int - hash code of the FileData.
Pre-condition: None.
Post-condition: Equal FileData objects have equal hash codes.

FileData::equals
Summary: Checks whether this FileData is equal to another object.
Algorithm:
  1. If o is this, return true.
  2. If o is not an instance of FileData, return false.
  3. Compare content using byte-array equality.
  4. Compare mimeType using object equality.
  5. Return true only if both fields are equal.
Related Features: content, mimeType
Related Classes: FileData, EByteArray, EJavaObject
Input: o : EJavaObject - the object to compare against.
Output: boolean - true if o is a FileData with equal content and mimeType.
Pre-condition: None.
Post-condition: Value equality is determined by content and mimeType only.

# Operation Specifications
FileData::hashCode
Summary: Computes a hash code for this FileData.
Algorithm:
  1. Compute a hash of the content byte array.
  2. Combine it with the hash of mimeType using e.g. 31 * contentHash + mimeTypeHash.
  3. Return the resulting int.
Related Features: content, mimeType
Related Classes: FileData, EByteArray
Input: None.
Output: int - hash code of the FileData.
Pre-condition: None.
Post-condition: Equal FileData objects have equal hash codes.

FileData::equals
Summary: Checks whether this FileData is equal to another object.
Algorithm:
  1. If o is this, return true.
  2. If o is not an instance of FileData, return false.
  3. Compare content using byte-array equality.
  4. Compare mimeType using object equality.
  5. Return true only if both fields are equal.
Related Features: content, mimeType
Related Classes: FileData, EByteArray, EJavaObject
Input: o : EJavaObject - the object to compare against.
Output: boolean - true if o is a FileData with equal content and mimeType.
Pre-condition: None.
Post-condition: Value equality is determined by content and mimeType only.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: FreddyRodgers_emojichef

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system encodes text and binary files into emoji strings and decodes them back to their original data. It treats emoji characters as symbols in a configurable base alphabet, allowing ordinary bytes to be represented as compact emoji sequences. The system also supports optional compression, optional integrity hashing, file MIME preservation, and simple file-oriented encode/decode workflows.

## 2. Functional Requirement

Recipe selection controls the emoji alphabet size. The supported recipe names are `quick`, `light`, `classic`, and `gourmet`, with increasing capacities of 64, 128, 256, and 1024 symbols. The classic recipe represents 8 bits per emoji. Unknown recipes should fall back to classic behavior.

Text encoding should use UTF-8 bytes, optionally compress the byte stream, split the resulting bits into recipe-sized chunks, and map each chunk to an emoji symbol. Decoding should reverse this process and reject null input or symbols that are not part of the active emoji alphabet. Empty text should round-trip as an empty emoji string.

Compression mode `zlib` should use standard zlib-compatible compression and decompression. Compression mode `none` should leave bytes unchanged. Verification mode `sha256` should produce a lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 digest with zero padding; verification mode `none` should produce an empty digest.

Binary data should be wrapped in a JSON-compatible structure before emoji encoding. The binary content is stored as Base64 text, and MIME type and original byte size must be preserved. The serialized field names are `content`, `mime_type`, and `size`. Decoding binary emoji data should restore the byte array and MIME type.

Encoding statistics should report `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `actual_ratio`, `theoretical_ratio`, and `bits_per_emoji`. File encoding results should include `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `mime_type`, and `actual_ratio`; file decoding results should include `decoded_size` and `mime_type`. File information results should include size, MIME type, whether the file can be processed, and a suggested recipe, or an error with `can_process` set to false.

Recipe suggestion is based on file size: small files below 1 KB use `quick`, files below 10 KB use `light`, files below 100 KB use `classic`, and larger files use `gourmet`.

File handling should read raw bytes, infer MIME type from the file name when possible, fall back to `application/octet-stream` when unknown, and write bytes while creating missing parent directories.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Operation Specifications
FileHandler::writeFile
Summary: Writes bytes to a file, creating missing parent directories.
Algorithm:
  1. Resolve filePath to a Path.
  2. If the parent directory is missing, create missing parent directories.
  3. Write content to the target path.
Related Features: none
Related Classes: EByteArray
Input: filePath : String - destination path; content : EByteArray - bytes to write.
Output: void.
Pre-condition: filePath is non-null; content is non-null.
Post-condition: The destination file exists and contains content; missing parent directories are created.

FileHandler::guessMimeType
Summary: Infers a MIME type from the file name extension.
Algorithm:
  1. Extract the file name from path.
  2. Extract the extension after the last dot and lower-case it.
  3. Look up the extension in an internal extension-to-MIME table (txt, html, json, png, jpg, pdf, zip, etc.).
  4. If the extension is known, return the corresponding MIME type.
  5. Otherwise, return "application/octet-stream".
Related Features: none
Related Classes: None
Input: path : Path - file path whose MIME type is guessed.
Output: String - MIME type, or "application/octet-stream" for unknown extensions.
Pre-condition: path is non-null.
Post-condition: A MIME type is returned; unknown files fall back to application/octet-stream.

FileHandler::readFile
Summary: Reads a file as raw bytes and determines its MIME type from the file name.
Algorithm:
  1. Resolve filePath to a Path.
  2. Read all bytes from that path.
  3. Call guessMimeType(path).
  4. Return createFileData(mimeType, bytes).
Related Features: guessMimeType, createFileData
Related Classes: FileData, EByteArray
Input: filePath : String - path of the file to read.
Output: FileData - object containing raw bytes and inferred MIME type.
Pre-condition: filePath is non-null and refers to a readable file.
Post-condition: The returned FileData contains the file's byte content and MIME type.

FileHandler::createFileData
Summary: Creates a new FileData object from the provided MIME type and content bytes.
Algorithm:
  1. Return new FileData(content, mimeType).
Related Features: none
Related Classes: FileData, EByteArray
Input: mimeType : String - MIME type; content : EByteArray - file bytes.
Output: FileData - new object holding both values.
Pre-condition: content is non-null; mimeType is non-null.
Post-condition: The returned FileData preserves the supplied content and MIME type.

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Infers a MIME type from the file name extension.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Extract the file name from path.
   *   2. Extract the extension after the last dot and lower-case it.
   *   3. Look up the extension in an internal extension-to-MIME table (txt, html, json, png, jpg, pdf, zip, etc.).
   *   4. If the extension is known, return the corresponding MIME type.
   *   5. Otherwise, return "application/octet-stream".
   * Related Features: none
   * Related Classes: None
   * Input: path : Path - file path whose MIME type is guessed.
   * Output: String - MIME type, or "application/octet-stream" for unknown extensions.
   * Pre-condition: path is non-null.
   * Post-condition: A MIME type is returned; unknown files fall back to application/octet-stream.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model pathDataType="edu.freddyrodger.Path"
   *        annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public String guessMimeType(final Path path)
  {
    String fileName = path.getFileName() == null ? "" : path.getFileName().toString();
    int dotIndex = fileName.lastIndexOf('.');
    if (dotIndex < 0 || dotIndex == fileName.length() - 1)
    {
      return "application/octet-stream";
    }
    String extension = fileName.substring(dotIndex + 1).toLowerCase();
    Map<String, String> mimeTypes = new HashMap<>();
    mimeTypes.put("txt", "text/plain");
    mimeTypes.put("html", "text/html");
    mimeTypes.put("htm", "text/html");
    mimeTypes.put("json", "application/json");
    mimeTypes.put("xml", "application/xml");
    mimeTypes.put("png", "image/png");
    mimeTypes.put("jpg", "image/jpeg");
    mimeTypes.put("jpeg", "image/jpeg");
    mimeTypes.put("gif", "image/gif");
    mimeTypes.put("pdf", "application/pdf");
    mimeTypes.put("zip", "application/zip");
    mimeTypes.put("css", "text/css");
    mimeTypes.put("js", "application/javascript");
    mimeTypes.put("csv", "text/csv");
    mimeTypes.put("mp3", "audio/mpeg");
    mimeTypes.put("mp4", "video/mp4");
    return mimeTypes.getOrDefault(extension, "application/octet-stream");
  }

   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
```java
   * Summary: Encodes UTF-8 text into an emoji string using the active recipe alphabet.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If data is null, reject.
   *   2. Convert data to raw UTF-8 bytes.
   *   3. If raw bytes are empty, return an empty emoji string.
   *   4. Call processData(raw) to optionally compress the byte stream.
   *   5. Prepend a 4-byte big-endian length prefix equal to the processed byte length.
   *   6. Convert the resulting payload to a bit stream, MSB first.
   *   7. Split the bit stream into bitsPerChunk-sized chunks, padding the final chunk with zero bits if needed.
   *   8. Map each chunk value through emojiMap and append the resulting emoji character.
   * Related Features: processData, emojiMap, bitsPerChunk, mask, baseSize, compression
   * Related Classes: EMap, EByteArray, CompressionMethod
   * Input: data : String - UTF-8 text to encode; must be non-null; empty allowed.
   * Output: String - emoji string using only active recipe symbols; empty for empty input.
   * Pre-condition: EmojiCodec is initialized; recipeType, compression mode, and maps are configured.
   * Post-condition: decode(encode(data)) equals data; empty text remains an empty emoji string.

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Reverses an emoji-encoded text string back into the original UTF-8 text.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If emojiData is null, reject.
   *   2. If emojiData is empty, return an empty string.
   *   3. For each Unicode code point (emoji symbol) in emojiData, look it up in reverseMap; reject if the symbol is absent.
   *   4. Convert each reverse-mapped chunk index to bitsPerChunk bits, MSB first, and concatenate them.
   *   5. Convert the concatenated bit stream to bytes, padding the final partial byte with zero bits if needed.
   *   6. Parse the first 4 bytes as a big-endian length prefix.
   *   7. Keep exactly that many processed bytes after the prefix and discard padding bytes.
   *   8. Call unprocessData on those processed bytes and return the resulting bytes as a UTF-8 string.
   * Related Features: reverseMap, bitsPerChunk, unprocessData
   * Related Classes: EMap, EByteArray, CompressionMethod
   * Input: emojiData : String - an emoji string to decode; null and invalid symbols are rejected; empty returns empty.
   * Output: String - the decoded UTF-8 text; empty if emojiData is empty.
   * Pre-condition: EmojiCodec is initialized.
   * Post-condition: If emojiData is null or contains a symbol absent from the active reverseMap, the operation rejects; otherwise, if emojiData was produced by encode, the original text is returned.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated
```

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.

4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

```java
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->

   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Encodes UTF-8 text into an emoji string using the active recipe alphabet.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If data is null, reject.
   *   2. Convert data to raw UTF-8 bytes.
   *   3. If raw bytes are empty, return an empty emoji string.
   *   4. Call processData(raw) to optionally compress the byte stream.
   *   5. Prepend a 4-byte big-endian length prefix equal to the processed byte length.
   *   6. Convert the resulting payload to a bit stream, MSB first.
   *   7. Split the bit stream into bitsPerChunk-sized chunks, padding the final chunk with zero bits if needed.
   *   8. Map each chunk value through emojiMap and append the resulting emoji character.
   * Related Features: processData, emojiMap, bitsPerChunk, mask, baseSize, compression
   * Related Classes: EMap, EByteArray, CompressionMethod
   * Input: data : String - UTF-8 text to encode; must be non-null; empty allowed.
   * Output: String - emoji string using only active recipe symbols; empty for empty input.
   * Pre-condition: EmojiCodec is initialized; recipeType, compression mode, and maps are configured.
   * Post-condition: decode(encode(data)) equals data; empty text remains an empty emoji string.

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Reverses an emoji-encoded text string back into the original UTF-8 text.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If emojiData is null, reject.
   *   2. If emojiData is empty, return an empty string.
   *   3. For each Unicode code point (emoji symbol) in emojiData, look it up in reverseMap; reject if the symbol is absent.
   *   4. Convert each reverse-mapped chunk index to bitsPerChunk bits, MSB first, and concatenate them.
   *   5. Convert the concatenated bit stream to bytes, padding the final partial byte with zero bits if needed.
   *   6. Parse the first 4 bytes as a big-endian length prefix.
   *   7. Keep exactly that many processed bytes after the prefix and discard padding bytes.
   *   8. Call unprocessData on those processed bytes and return the resulting bytes as a UTF-8 string.
   * Related Features: reverseMap, bitsPerChunk, unprocessData
   * Related Classes: EMap, EByteArray, CompressionMethod
   * Input: emojiData : String - an emoji string to decode; null and invalid symbols are rejected; empty returns empty.
   * Output: String - the decoded UTF-8 text; empty if emojiData is empty.
   * Pre-condition: EmojiCodec is initialized.
   * Post-condition: If emojiData is null or contains a symbol absent from the active reverseMap, the operation rejects; otherwise, if emojiData was produced by encode, the original text is returned.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated
```

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

```java
   * Summary: Encodes UTF-8 text into an emoji string using the active recipe alphabet.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If data is null, reject.
   *   2. Convert data to raw UTF-8 bytes.
   *   3. If raw bytes are empty, return an empty emoji string.

   *   7. Split the bit stream into bitsPerChunk-sized chunks, padding the final chunk with zero bits if needed.

   *   1. If emojiData is null, reject.

   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
```
```java
   * Summary: Encodes UTF-8 text into an emoji string using the active recipe alphabet.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If data is null, reject.
   *   2. Convert data to raw UTF-8 bytes.
   *   3. If raw bytes are empty, return an empty emoji string.
   *   4. Call processData(raw) to optionally compress the byte stream.
   *   5. Prepend a 4-byte big-endian length prefix equal to the processed byte length.

   *   7. Split the bit stream into bitsPerChunk-sized chunks, padding the final chunk with zero bits if needed.
   *   8. Map each chunk value through emojiMap and append the resulting emoji character.
```
